-- AAS-SECTIONS (2026-07-17): p_sections on admin_analytics_stats — the DO-NOW subset of
-- the deferred D3-7c decision (docs/D3-7c_MONEY_PREAGG_PROPOSAL.md). The analytics RPC is
-- the app's slowest interactive call (737-807ms, 12 sections; profitability ~300ms +
-- sellers ~250ms + mediabuyers ~80ms). Exact D3-7 dashboard pattern: each section
-- IF-gated on p_sections (SQL untouched), conditional return object; default NULL = all
-- 12 (existing callers unaffected). The page splits into a FAST call (9 sections, 128ms
-- measured) that paints immediately + a HEAVY call (3 sections, 637ms) that streams in.
-- PARITY PROVEN pre-apply (rolled-back, impersonated admin): 12/12 checks over 4 windows —
-- v2(null) byte-equals old; fast||heavy reassembles the full payload exactly; key counts
-- exact. Signature change -> DROP+CREATE; ACL restored (authenticated+service_role, NO
-- anon — watch the default-privileges auto-grant, revoked below).

BEGIN;

DROP FUNCTION public.admin_analytics_stats(uuid, date, date);

CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.admin_analytics_stats(p_business uuid, p_from date DEFAULT NULL::date, p_to date DEFAULT NULL::date, p_sections text[] DEFAULT NULL)
 RETURNS jsonb
 LANGUAGE plpgsql
 STABLE
 SET search_path TO ''
AS $function$
declare
    v_now       timestamptz := now();
    v_today     date := (now() at time zone 'Africa/Casablanca')::date;
    v_start     timestamptz := case when p_from is not null
                                    then p_from::timestamp at time zone 'Africa/Casablanca' end;
    v_end_excl  timestamptz := case when p_to is not null
                                    then (p_to + 1)::timestamp at time zone 'Africa/Casablanca' end;
    v_has_win   boolean := (p_from is not null or p_to is not null);
        ), '[]'::jsonb)
    )
    into v_users;
    end if;

        -- Only computed sections are present; default (null) returns all 12.
    if v_profitability is not null then v_out := v_out || jsonb_build_object('profitability', v_profitability); end if;
    if v_health is not null then v_out := v_out || jsonb_build_object('health', v_health); end if;
    if v_orders is not null then v_out := v_out || jsonb_build_object('orders', v_orders); end if;
    if v_ads is not null then v_out := v_out || jsonb_build_object('ads', v_ads); end if;
    if v_shipping is not null then v_out := v_out || jsonb_build_object('shipping', v_shipping); end if;
    if v_inventory is not null then v_out := v_out || jsonb_build_object('inventory', v_inventory); end if;
    if v_channels is not null then v_out := v_out || jsonb_build_object('channels', v_channels); end if;
    if v_customers is not null then v_out := v_out || jsonb_build_object('customers', v_customers); end if;
    if v_sellers is not null then v_out := v_out || jsonb_build_object('sellers', v_sellers); end if;
    if v_couriers is not null then v_out := v_out || jsonb_build_object('couriers', v_couriers); end if;
    if v_mediabuyers is not null then v_out := v_out || jsonb_build_object('mediabuyers', v_mediabuyers); end if;
    if v_users is not null then v_out := v_out || jsonb_build_object('users', v_users); end if;
    return v_out;
end;
$function$
;

REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.admin_analytics_stats(uuid, date, date, text[]) FROM PUBLIC;
REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.admin_analytics_stats(uuid, date, date, text[]) FROM anon;
GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.admin_analytics_stats(uuid, date, date, text[]) TO authenticated, service_role;

NOTIFY pgrst, 'reload schema';

COMMIT;

     1	-- Security remediation 2026-07-03 (self-owned self-hosted production box).
     2	-- Enforces column/row/tenant authorization controls surfaced by the internal
     3	-- secure-coding review. All changes are non-destructive to data.
     4	--
     5	-- Design note on the guard triggers (R2/R5/R6): they are SECURITY INVOKER so
     6	-- that current_user reflects the ACTUAL executing role. This lets them exempt
     7	-- non-end-user writers cleanly:
     8	--   * service_role  -> manage-users / provider webhooks / admin edge flows
     9	--   * postgres      -> SECURITY DEFINER fns (sync_imported_orders, approve_sheet_order)
    10	--   * cron          -> scheduled jobs
    11	-- Only a real `authenticated` self-write is gated. (A SECURITY DEFINER trigger
    12	-- would see current_user='postgres' and auth.uid()=NULL for service_role, which
    13	-- would WRONGLY block legitimate admin edits made through manage-users.)
    14	
    15	BEGIN;
    16	
    17	-- ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    18	-- Shared helper: is the acting user a business admin?
    19	-- ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    20	CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.current_user_is_business_admin()
    21	RETURNS boolean
    22	LANGUAGE sql STABLE SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = public
    23	AS $$
    24	  SELECT EXISTS (
    25	    SELECT 1 FROM public.users u
    26	    WHERE u.id = auth.uid() AND u.role IN ('super admin','admin')
    27	  );
    28	$$;
    29	REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.current_user_is_business_admin() FROM public;
    30	GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.current_user_is_business_admin() TO authenticated;
    31	
    32	-- ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    33	-- R2 — users: column-level UPDATE grant + privileged-column guard
    34	-- ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    35	REVOKE UPDATE ON public.users FROM authenticated;
    36	GRANT UPDATE (
    37	  name, username, phone, address,
    38	  avatar_url, avatar_config,
    39	  bank_name, bank_account_holder, bank_rib, cin_number,
    40	  courier_parcel_fragile, courier_parcel_open, courier_parcel_try_on,
    41	  updated_at
    42	) ON public.users TO authenticated;
    43	
    44	CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.guard_users_self_privileged_cols()
    45	RETURNS trigger
    46	LANGUAGE plpgsql SET search_path = public, pg_temp
    47	AS $$
    48	BEGIN
    49	  -- Exempt non-end-user writers (see header). Only gate real self-edits.
    50	  IF current_user <> 'authenticated' THEN RETURN NEW; END IF;
    51	  IF public.current_user_is_business_admin() THEN RETURN NEW; END IF;
    52	
    53	  IF ( NEW.role              IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.role
    54	    OR NEW."DigiCom Business" IS DISTINCT FROM OLD."DigiCom Business"
    55	    OR NEW.permissions       IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.permissions

    62	      USING ERRCODE = '42501';

    64	  RETURN NEW;

// @ts-nocheck
// Admin-gated: activate / deactivate a user account, then email account_reactivated /
// account_suspended (with the admin-supplied reason). The status flip is the primary action;
// the email is best-effort (a mail failure won't undo the flip). Caller must be a super admin /
// admin in the SAME business as the target user.
import "jsr:@supabase/functions-js/edge-runtime.d.ts";
import { createClient } from "https://esm.sh/@supabase/supabase-js@2.39.3";

const corsHeaders = {
  "Access-Control-Allow-Origin": "*",
  "Access-Control-Allow-Headers": "authorization, x-client-info, apikey, content-type",
};
const json = (o: unknown, s: number) =>
  new Response(JSON.stringify(o), { headers: { ...corsHeaders, "Content-Type": "application/json" }, status: s });

const ROLE_FR: Record<string, string> = {
  "super admin": "Super administrateur", "business owner": "Propriétaire d'entreprise", "admin": "Administrateur", "commercial": "Commercial",
  "courier": "Livreur", "mediabuyer": "Media buyer", "owner": "Propriétaire",
};
const BIZ_OWNER_PROTECTED_MSG = "Le propriétaire de l'entreprise ne peut être modifié que par la plateforme.";

Deno.serve(async (req) => {
  if (req.method === "OPTIONS") return new Response("ok", { headers: corsHeaders });

  try {
    const url = Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_URL") ?? "";
    const SERVICE = Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY") ?? "";
    const service = createClient(url, SERVICE, { auth: { persistSession: false, autoRefreshToken: false } });

    const jwt = (req.headers.get("Authorization") ?? "").replace(/^bearer\s+/i, "").trim();
    const { data: { user }, error: userErr } = await service.auth.getUser(jwt);
    if (userErr || !user) return json({ ok: false, reason: "not_authenticated" }, 401);

    const { data: me } = await service.from("users").select('role, "DigiCom Business", active').eq("id", user.id).single();
    if (!me) return json({ ok: false, reason: "profile_not_found" }, 403);
    if (me.active === false) return json({ ok: false, reason: "account_disabled" }, 403);
    if (!["super admin", "business owner", "admin"].includes(String(me.role))) return json({ ok: false, reason: "forbidden" }, 403);
    const businessId = me["DigiCom Business"];
    if (!businessId) return json({ ok: false, reason: "no_business" }, 400);

    const body = await req.json().catch(() => ({}));
    const userId = String(body?.user_id ?? "");
    if (!userId) return json({ ok: false, reason: "missing_user" }, 400);
    const active = body?.active === true;
    let reason = String(body?.reason ?? "").trim();
    const origin = typeof body?.origin === "string" && /^https?:\/\//.test(body.origin)
      ? body.origin.replace(/\/$/, "") : (Deno.env.get("SITE_URL") || "https://igrowfast.com").replace(/\/$/, "");

    // Target must belong to the caller's business.
    const { data: target } = await service.from("users")
      .select('id, name, email, role, "DigiCom Business"').eq("id", userId).maybeSingle();
    if (!target) return json({ ok: false, reason: "not_found" }, 404);
    if (target["DigiCom Business"] !== businessId) return json({ ok: false, reason: "forbidden" }, 403);
    if (userId === user.id) return json({ ok: false, reason: "cannot_change_self" }, 400);
    if (target.role === "business owner" && me.role !== "owner") {
      return json({ ok: false, reason: "forbidden", error: BIZ_OWNER_PROTECTED_MSG }, 403);
    }

    // Primary action: flip the status (mirrors the previous direct update, now server-side).
    const { error: updErr } = await service.from("users").update({ active }).eq("id", userId);
    if (updErr) return json({ ok: false, reason: "update_failed", detail: updErr.message }, 400);

    // Best-effort notification.
    let emailWarning: string | null = null;
    if (target.email) {
      const { data: biz } = await service.from("businesses").select("name").eq("id", businessId).maybeSingle();
      const nowFr = (() => {
        try { return new Date().toLocaleString("fr-FR", { timeZone: "Africa/Casablanca", dateStyle: "long", timeStyle: "short" }); }
        catch { return new Date().toISOString(); }
      })();
      if (!reason) reason = active ? "Votre accès a été rétabli par l'administrateur." : "Non précisé.";
      const key = active ? "account_reactivated" : "account_suspended";
      const vars: Record<string, unknown> = {
        user_name: target.name || "",
        role_label: ROLE_FR[target.role] || String(target.role),
        business_name: biz?.name || "votre entreprise",
        reason,
      };
      if (active) { vars.reactivated_at = nowFr; vars.login_url = `${origin}/auth`; }
      else { vars.suspended_at = nowFr; }

      try {
        const resp = await fetch(`${url}/functions/v1/send-email`, {
          method: "POST",
          headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json", "Authorization": `Bearer ${SERVICE}`, "apikey": SERVICE },
          body: JSON.stringify({ key, to: target.email, user_id: userId, vars }),
        });
        const out = await resp.json().catch(() => ({}));
        if (!resp.ok || !out?.ok) emailWarning = out?.detail || out?.error || `status ${resp.status}`;
      } catch (e) {
        emailWarning = (e as Error).message;
      }
    }

    return json({ ok: true, active, email_warning: emailWarning }, 200);
  } catch (e) {
    return json({ ok: false, reason: "error", detail: (e as Error).message }, 500);
  }
});
      }
      try {
        await applyGaAccess(supabaseClient, authData.user.id, ga_access);
        await applyMetaAccess(supabaseClient, authData.user.id, meta_access);
      } catch (accErr) {
        console.error("applyGa/MetaAccess (create) failed:", accErr);
      }

      return new Response(JSON.stringify(profileData), {
        headers: { ...corsHeaders, "Content-Type": "application/json" },
        status: 200,
      });
    }

    if (action === "update") {
      // Same rule as create: only the backup_root caller may set backup_admin;
      // backup_root itself is never accepted from the client.
      const { email, password, whatsapp_access, ga_access, meta_access, backup_admin, backup_root: _backupRootIgnored, ...rest } = userData;
      if (me.backup_root === true && typeof backup_admin === "boolean") {
        rest.backup_admin = backup_admin;
      }

      if (me.role !== "owner") {
        const { data: targetUser } = await supabaseClient
          .from("users").select("role").eq("id", userId).maybeSingle();
        if (targetUser?.role === "business owner" || rest.role === "business owner") {
          return json({ error: BIZ_OWNER_PROTECTED_MSG }, 403);
        }
      }

      // Read the current auth login email so we only touch GoTrue when something
      // actually changed. Forwarding an unchanged email made GoTrue treat every
      // edit (even password-only) as an email change and reject it with 422.
      let currentAuthEmail: string | null = null;
      {
        const { data: cur, error: curErr } = await supabaseClient.auth.admin.getUserById(userId);
        if (curErr) throw friendlyError(curErr);
        currentAuthEmail = cur?.user?.email ?? null;
      }

      const submittedEmail = typeof email === "string" ? email.trim() : "";
      const emailChanged = submittedEmail.length > 0 &&
        submittedEmail.toLowerCase() !== (currentAuthEmail ?? "").toLowerCase();

      const authUpdates: Record<string, unknown> = {};
      if (emailChanged) {
        authUpdates.email = submittedEmail;
        authUpdates.email_confirm = true; // admin-initiated change, no confirmation round-trip
      }
      if (password) authUpdates.password = password;

      if (Object.keys(authUpdates).length > 0) {
        const { error: authError } = await supabaseClient.auth.admin.updateUserById(
          userId,
          authUpdates,
        );
        if (authError) throw friendlyError(authError, emailChanged ? "email" : "password");
      }

      // auth.users is the source of truth for the login email; the profile email
      // always mirrors it. This both prevents and self-heals drift on every edit.

hook: PreToolUse Completed

     3	-- Two defects on one table:

     6	-- (2) DATA BUG (Tier-3): status CHECK was ('open','in_progress','resolved','closed') but the

     8	--     (admin/Demands.tsx) → every insert + dismiss 400s → the feature is fully broken (0 rows).

// IGF IDENTITY ADAPTER — replaces WA Mancer's lib/auth.tsx (sanctioned seam #3).
//
// WA Mancer shipped a full AuthProvider (profiles table + localStorage cache).
// iGrowFast owns identity (public.users, 6-role model, ProtectedRoute guards),
// so this module keeps ONLY the consumer-facing contract the inbox files were
// built against — same import path ('../lib/auth'), same useAuth() shape:
//
//   { session, profile, loading, profileLoading, revalidating,
//     signIn, signOut, refreshProfile }
//
// profile is the WA `Profile` row shape (whatsapp.profiles view contract),
// mapped from public.users:
//   full_name    <- users.name
//   role         <- {'super admin','business owner','admin'} -> 'admin', else 'user'
//                   (WA user_role value space; every UI gate compares === 'admin',
//                    parity with public.current_user_is_business_admin())
//   is_active    <- users.active
//   avatar_config passthrough (public.users.avatar_config jsonb)
//   avatar_url   passthrough (public.users.avatar_url — uploaded photo; IGF
//                 precedence photo → config → fallback, see UserAvatar.tsx)
//
// No provider needed: session comes from the single IGF GoTrue client, the
// profile row through TanStack Query (WaShell's waQueryClient). Consumers all
// null-guard (`session?.user.id`, `profile?.role`), so the brief async gap on
// first mount is safe.
import { useCallback, useEffect, useState } from 'react'
import type { Session } from '@supabase/supabase-js'
import { useQuery, useQueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query'
import { supabase } from './supabase'
import type { Profile } from './database.types'

const PROFILE_KEY = ['wa-current-profile'] as const

// Module-level session mirror so remounts render with the last-known session
// immediately instead of a null flash on every navigation.
let cachedSession: Session | null = null

function mapUsersRow(row: {
  id: string
  name: string | null
  email: string | null
  role: string | null
  active: boolean | null
  avatar_config: unknown
  avatar_url: string | null
  created_at: string | null
}): Profile {
  return {
    id: row.id,
    email: row.email ?? '',
    full_name: row.name,
    role: row.role === 'super admin' || row.role === 'business owner' || row.role === 'admin' ? 'admin' : 'user',
    is_active: row.active ?? true,
    avatar_config: (row.avatar_config ?? null) as Profile['avatar_config'],
    avatar_url: row.avatar_url ?? null,
    created_at: row.created_at ?? '',
    last_auto_assigned_at: null,
    updated_at: row.created_at ?? '',
  }
}

async function fetchProfile(userId: string): Promise<Profile | null> {
  const { data, error } = await supabase
    .from('users')
    .select('id, name, email, role, active, avatar_config, avatar_url, created_at')
    .eq('id', userId)
    .maybeSingle()
  if (error) {
    console.warn('wa profile fetch error', error)
    return null
  }
  return data ? mapUsersRow(data) : null
}

export function useAuth() {
  const qc = useQueryClient()
  const [session, setSession] = useState<Session | null>(cachedSession)
  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(cachedSession === null)

  useEffect(() => {
    let cancelled = false
    void supabase.auth.getSession().then(({ data }) => {
      if (cancelled) return
      cachedSession = data.session
      setSession(data.session)
      setLoading(false)
    })
    const { data: sub } = supabase.auth.onAuthStateChange((_event, next) => {
      if (cancelled) return
      cachedSession = next

Default is no-training at both (Anthropic Commercial Terms §B *\"may not train\"*; OpenAI §4.2 + API since 2023-03-01). **No config change is needed to stop training — only to accidentally start it.** |\n| **T3.16** | **DECISION** | **DPF contingency SCCs for Meta / Google / Snap** | ***Latombe v Commission* (T-553/23), General Court, 3 Sept 2025** — dismissed in its entirety, DPF held \"essentially equivalent\". **But an appeal to the CJEU remains possible and NOYB has signalled further challenges.** Keep SCCs documented as fallback so an invalidation is a **contract swap, not an outage**. |\n| **T3.17** | **DECISION** | **Plan for NO one-stop-shop** | No EU establishment → no Art 56 lead authority (EDPB 3/2018 p.13), **and the OSS never applies to ePrivacy at all** (Taskforce ¶4). Every Member State's DPA can act individually; breach notifications go to **each** affected SA via the rep. |\n\n## TIER 4 — MEDIUM / HOUSEKEEPING\n\n| # | Type | Item | Detail |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **T4.1** | **FILING** | **Wyoming annual report** | Due the **first day of the anniversary month** → **1 July** each year; **first due 1 July 2027**. License tax = greater of **$60** or **$0.0002 per dollar of WY-situs assets**. |\n| **T4.2** | **VERIFY** | **Registered-agent registered-office validity** | WY SoS: *\"post office boxes, drop boxes, virtual addresses, mail forwarding locations, UPS or FedEx stores **do not qualify** as an acceptable registered office address.\"* **30 N Gould St Ste R is a mass-registration suite.** Presumably it is the commercial RA's own registered office (legitimate) — **confirm with the registered agent.** A defective registered office risks **administrative dissolution**. |\n| **T4.3** | **OPS** | **Sub-processor change subscriptions** | Anthropic **~15 days** (`anthropic.com/subprocessors`), OpenAI **30 days** (`platform.openai.com/subprocessors`). Propagate to seller-clients under their DPA. |\n| **T4.4** | **PRODUCT** | **Feedback control for problematic AI outputs** | Route genuine model-safety issues to `usersafety@anthropic.com`. |\n| **T4.5** | **OPS** | **Anthropic publicity opt-out (§G)** | Anthropic may use **iGrowFast's** name/logo to identify it publicly as a customer. Grants iGrowFast **nothing** reciprocal. Opt out if undesirable. |\n| **T4.6** | **OPS** | **Monitored non-personal platform contact addresses** | TikTok: *\"Failure to respond to correspondence in a timely manner can lead to termination of your integration.\"* Shopify requires a **separate emergency developer contact** in the Partner Dashboard. Must not be founder-identifying. |\n| **T4.7** | **PRODUCT** | **Woo technical gate** | **HPOS compatibility mandatory for all new submissions**; WordPress Accessibility Coding Standards; PHP 7.4+; block editor; must not break widely-used Marketplace extensions. Slug: company name may not lead (\"Product Addons for WooCommerce\" ✅ / \"WooCommerce – Product Addons\" ❌). |\n| **T4.8** | **OPS** | **Shopify review-solicitation rules** | Neutral language only; **no incentives**; no withholding features for reviews; no collecting reviews *\"through the app or external channels.\"* Also: no app whose primary purpose is to **migrate merchants off Shopify**; no using the API to *\"substantially replicate products or services offered by Shopify\"* (avoid \"replace your Shopify admin\" framing). |\n| **T4.9** | **VERIFY** | **TDPSA / SBA size standard** | **TDPSA has NO volume or revenue threshold** — only the **SBA small-business exemption** stands between iGrowFast and Texas coverage, and *\"coverage attaches instantly on outgrowing it, with no ramp.\"* Confirm and document the receipts-based standard for NAICS **513210 / 541511 / 518210** (~$34M–$47M range) at `sba.gov/size-standards`. **Re-check annually.** |\n| **T4.10** | **VERIFY** | **ICO data protection fee** | DPA 2018 s.137 + Charges & Information Regs 2018. **The ICO's public FAQs do not cleanly resolve** whether a non-UK controller with no UK establishment, in scope only via targeting, must pay. **Do not assume exemption. Confirm with the ICO directly before UK launch.** A filing + annual fee, not a page. |\n| **T4.11** | **VERIFY** | **FinCEN BOI** | **Medium confidence:** FinCEN's interim final rule of ~26 Mar 2025 exempted **US-formed** companies, leaving only foreign reporting companies obligated. **Confirm at `fincen.gov/boi`.** If it applies it is a **filing, never public** — no impact on the founder-anonymity constraint. |\n| **T4.12** | **VERIFY** | **Three unretrieved platform documents** | **(a)** TikTok **Business Products (Data) Terms** — login-walled at `ads.tiktok.com/i18n/official/policy/business-products-terms`; **may contain a concrete retention day-count and a privacy-policy disclosure duty.** **(b)** TikTok Shop Partner Center Developer ToS + data-security review — `partner.tiktokshop.com` served truncated. **(c)** **Snap Brand Guidelines PDF** (Sept 2025) at `snap.com/brand-guidelines` — **the actual logo rules live in the PDF, not the HTML.** Pull all three via an authenticated session **before** signing off on retention commitments or any Snap logo usage. |\n| **T4.13** | **NON-ISSUE — confirmed, do not spend money here** | Supported regions: **Morocco and the US are supported by BOTH** Anthropic and OpenAI. **Neither trains on API data by default.** **Anthropic's DPA needs no signature** (auto-incorporated via Commercial Terms §C). **COD is not a \"high-risk use case\"** under either AI policy, and AI Act high-risk (2027/2028) does not reach it. **Wyoming imposes no website or disclosure requirements** — no comprehensive privacy law (2022 HB0086, 2024 HB0163, 2025 SF0065 all failed); the breach law (W.S. §40-12-501 *et seq.*) triggers on **WY residents, not on being a WY LLC** → dormant. **COD is not prohibited by WhatsApp** — the policy does not address payment method at all. |\n\n---\n\n# 4. THE TOP 10 THINGS THAT WILL ACTUALLY BACKFIRE\n\nRanked by **probability × severity × irreversibility**. Blunt.\n\n### 1. Netting commission out of collected COD — or shipping a \"wallet\"\n**Loi 103-12 Art 183: 6 months–3 years imprisonment + 100,000–5,000,000 MAD.** The instant COD proceeds hit an iGrowFast account, or commission is netted from collected cash, or a seller can draw down an on-platform balance, you are an **unlicensed établissement de paiement** performing *\"opérations de transfert de fonds\"* as *\"intermédiaire entre le payeur et le fournisseur de biens et services\"*, *à titre de profession habituelle*. **And iGrowFast LLC structurally cannot cure it**: Art 35-2 requires a **société anonyme or SARL** — Moroccan forms. A Wyoming LLC is neither. The cure is a Moroccan subsidiary + BAM capital + business plan + BAM review = **12–24 months, capital-intensive.** Note the severity gap: **3 years vs. 1 year, 5M MAD vs. 400k — this is an order of magnitude worse than the entire data-protection schedule combined.** And **Art 182 makes the marketing copy itself the offence** (3 mo–1 yr + 20k–200k) — a \"wallet\" UI or \"we pay your sellers\" headline is punishable **before a single dirham moves**. Free to comply with today. Catastrophic and unfixable if breached. **This is the item to get right before anything else on this page.**\n\n### 2. The US leg of your own architecture is already illegal in Morocco, and no page fixes it\nThe **USA is absent** from the CNDP adequacy list (Délibération 236-2015 — Germany is on it; the US is not). **iGrowFast LLC's own US-based admin/support access to Moroccan personal data is itself a transfer to a non-adequate state.** Read that again: **the company's American nationality creates the violation.** Being a US entity is a **liability** here, not a shield. Add Anthropic, OpenAI, Meta, TikTok, Snap, GA, Meta Ads, Shopify. **No Art 44 derogation is available at scale** — express consent is impossible (the sellers' customers never interact with you), and Art 44-1(d) contract-necessity collapses for AI and ad platforms because those are not *necessary* to execute the sale. Only **Art 44-3** (express, reasoned CNDP authorization) covers it, it is **not self-executing** (SCCs are not an instrument in Morocco), it takes 2 months, and it **presupposes an approved déclaration that does not exist**. **Price this correctly: the fines are not the risk.** Art 60 → 400k MAD (~USD 40k) is noise. The risks are **(i) personal criminal liability of directors** (Art 64 — *\"sans préjudice des peines qui peuvent être appliquées à ses dirigeants\"*), **(ii) a court order to erase the production database** (Art 61), **(iii) CNDP's public naming register** (live since 31 Mar 2023 — see the **TLSCONTACT** precedent: hearing 6 Jan 2023, compliance deadline 28 Feb 2023, Arts 52 and 60 invoked, referral to competent authorities), and **(iv) contamination of your seller base** — who are the Art 2-2(a) controllers with **Moroccan assets to seize**. A CNDP enforcement wave against sellers makes iGrowFast the reason their clients got fined. That kills the company commercially long before any criminal process concludes.\n\n### 3. Launching the marketing site with ad pixels and no consent gate\n**You are currently out of GDPR scope for the thing that actually matters** — Moroccan end-customers are not in the Union, so their names, phones, addresses and order contents are outside GDPR **no matter where you host them** (nationality is irrelevant; **location at the moment of the trigger activity** is what counts). And renting Contabo VPSs does **not** create an establishment — the Directive 95/46 \"use of equipment\" criterion was **deliberately deleted** from the GDPR, and EDPB Example 7 is your fact pattern. **Then you put a Meta Pixel on the homepage and hand it all away.** Art 3(2)(b) monitoring has **no intent requirement** (unlike 3(2)(a)); retargeting on an EU-reachable site *is* monitoring. That single tag triggers: Art 27 EU rep, a **separate** UK rep, the full Art 13 stack — **and Guidelines 05/2021 fn.15 makes YOU the exporter** (*\"personal data disclosed via cookies are… a transmission by the operator of the website\"* — you cannot hide behind \"the user's browser sent it\"). **Multiplied by: no establishment = no one-stop-shop under GDPR, and the OSS never applies to ePrivacy at all.** You face **27 supervisory authorities individually, with no lead-authority shield, and no way to buy one** without an EU establishment that would itself trigger Art 3(1) for everything. This is entirely self-inflicted, entirely preventable, and costs one configuration decision.\n\n### 4. TCPA\n**$500 per message, trebled to $1,500 for willful/knowing, per message, no statutory cap, private right of action, the most-classed statute in the US.** 100,000 non-compliant messages = **$50M–$150M nominal**. \"Willful\" under the Communications Act means **a conscious act, irrespective of intent to violate** — a low bar. The WhatsApp-is-not-a-\"call\" argument is **probably right and completely unadjudicated**: *\"treat this as an argument you would win, not a rule you can build a business on.\"* Any **SMS fallback, OTP, or carrier-network message is squarely in scope**. **The indemnity is worthless** — it is paperwork from a judgment-proof Moroccan micro-seller, and under the FCC's 2013 *DISH Network* ruling a platform that supplies templates, schedules sends, optimizes timing, or auto-triggers messages on order events **looks like the \"maker/initiator\"** and is liable **directly**, plus **vicariously** under agency (actual authority, apparent authority, ratification). **The engineering controls are the defense.** And the **cross-subject-matter revocation prong went live 11 April 2026**: an opt-out on a **delivery notification** must now kill **marketing**. Also live: state **mini-TCPAs** (FL, OK, MD, WA CEMA, NJ), several drafted around **\"text message\"** rather than \"call\" — which **routes around *Steidinger* entirely**. And do not over-read *Steidinger v. Blackstone* (7th Cir., 14 Jul 2026): it kills the **§227(c)(5)** DNC private action for texts **only**; **§227(b)** — where the class-action money is — is untouched, and *Howard v. RNC* (9th Cir., 13 Jan 2026) went the other way. **Live circuit split.** Separately, the **highest-probability** Moroccan trigger in the whole stack sits adjacent: WhatsApp broadcast = **prospection directe** (Art 59) — *\"recipients notice it directly.\"*\n\n### 5. Shipping AI features over customer / WhatsApp content\nFour regimes fire simultaneously. **Morocco Art 12-1(b)**: sending order data and message content to Anthropic/OpenAI is *\"l'utilisation de données à caractère personnel à d'autres fins que celles pour lesquelles elles ont été collectées\"* → **AUTORISATION (F112, 2 months), not a déclaration** — *\"the single most under-appreciated trigger in the stack\"* — plus **Art 54 criminal exposure** (3 mo–1 yr + 20k–200k, doubled). **Shopify PPA**: absolute ban on using Merchant/Customer Data — *\"or enabling third parties to use\"* it — to train, fine-tune or improve any ML/AI system. **Meta Platform Terms**: solely for clients' purposes, **segregated**, no cross-client features. **GDPR Art 28(10)**: *\"if a processor infringes this Regulation by determining the purposes and means of processing, the processor shall be considered to be a controller\"* → you have breached the DPA **and you have no legal basis**. Stacked on top: **Anthropic's DPA hard-codes you as controller** when you are a processor, and **AI Act Art 50(1) disclosure binds from 2 August 2026** — **not delayed by the Digital Omnibus**, whatever the delay headlines said. And **message content will incidentally contain health, religious and political data** — *\"a COD platform processing raw message bodies cannot credibly assert it never touches sensitive data\"* (Art 12-1(a)), on which **there is no Moroccan doctrine at all** (UNCERTAIN).\n\n### 6. Publishing a retention schedule or a rights portal you haven't built\n**Both CPPA orders were mechanism failures, not policy-text failures.** **Todd Snyder — $345,178**: a misconfigured privacy portal silently dropped opt-outs **for 40 days**. **Honda — $632,500**: verification demanded before an opt-out, asymmetric choices, agent friction. **Nomi**: promised an in-store opt-out that never existed — the FTC did not allege Nomi lied about *what* it collected; **it lied about a control it offered.** *\"A 'Do Not Sell' link that doesn't work is worse than no link.\"* The same logic destroys a retention table that your **backups, your R2 media store, your blind-HMAC keyword index, and your AI vendors' logs** quietly contradict — that is simultaneously a **§5 deception** and an **Art 5(2) accountability failure**, and WP260 sets the bar at *\"phrased in a way that allows the data subject to assess, on the basis of his or her own situation, what the retention period will be.\"* **Your `/privacy#retention` table is the single sentence on this website most likely to be false on the day it ships.** Ship the deletion jobs first, then the table.\n\n### 7. Any US-hosting implication, on a site whose servers are in Germany\n**The entire marketing positioning is \"a US company.\"** That makes the US-hosting implication **the highest-probability §5 trap for this specific company** — a reader's default inference, not a claim you have to make. And it is false: VPSs are in **Germany (Contabo)**; the messaging vendor is **EU-hosted**. The precedent is exact and expensive: **GoodRx $1.5M**, **BetterHelp $7.8M**, **Avast $16.5M** (banned from selling browsing data; **$15.3M paid to 103,152 consumers in Dec 2025**) — all policies that **misdescribed data flows and third parties**. Adjacent and equally cheap to prove: calling **30 N Gould St Ste R** — *\"one of the most heavily-used mass registered-agent addresses in the US\"* — \"our headquarters\", or claiming a \"US-based team/support\" that does not exist. **A competitor or a journalist proves that in one search.** And understand the real penalty: **§5 alone carries no first-violation fine** (and after *AMG Capital* (2021) the FTC cannot get equitable monetary relief under §13(b)) — the punishment is an **injunction, a 20-year consent order, deletion and algorithmic disgorgement, and reputational kill.** The 20-year order is the sentence. Fix: **state the German hosting plainly.** *\"The mismatch is only damaging when it looks concealed.\"*\n\n### 8. A WhatsApp logo, a UI clone, or an \"Official Partner\" badge — on the page the Meta reviewer opens while deciding\n**Business Verification is a hard gate before App Review**, and the **website is itself a submitted verification field**. So the reviewer reads your marketing site while deciding whether to trust you. On it, pre-approval, they find: a partner badge you never earned, the WhatsApp glyph you have no licence to display (*\"the guidelines don't grant third parties a general marketing licence to display the logo\"* — the nominative carve-out **explicitly withholds it**), and a pixel-faithful WhatsApp-chrome clone in `#25D366`. That is **the exact \"conveys a relationship that doesn't exist\" pattern**, on the exact page, at the exact moment. Compounding it: **\"built on the WhatsApp Business Platform\" / \"official Cloud API\"** is **false today** because messaging runs through a third-party provider — *\"a reviewer who sees an unapproved applicant already claiming official-API status reads it as either misrepresentation or evidence of unofficial-access use — the latter being an independent basis for denial.\"* And **it costs you nothing commercially to avoid**: build the demo in your own design language — which is what you're actually selling — and say \"Works with WhatsApp\" in text. *\"This is the claim most likely to appear on a founder-written landing page — kill it now.\"* Separately, the same third-party stack is arguably an **Anthropic AUP breach today** (*\"circumvent the guardrails or terms of other platforms\"*) regardless of what the website says.\n\n### 9. Subscription billing without the ROSCA stack\n**Click-to-cancel (16 CFR Part 425) was VACATED IN ITS ENTIRETY by the Eighth Circuit on 8 July 2025 — six days before its compliance date. It never took effect.** Do not read that as safety. The grounds were **procedural** (the FTC skipped the preliminary regulatory analysis after its own ALJ found >$100M impact); **the substantive policy was not repudiated**, and an **ANPRM went to OIRA on 30 January 2026**. Meanwhile **ROSCA (15 U.S.C. §8403) is fully in force** and is the live vehicle: **Amazon — $2.5B** (25 Sep 2025; $1B civil penalty + $1.5B redress to ~35M consumers; **two named executives, an SVP and a VP, personally charged**); **Chegg — $7.5M**; **FTC v. Uber** pending. **All three post-date or bracket the vacatur.** A ROSCA violation is treated as an **FTC rule violation → $53,088 per violation + redress + injunction.** Then **California's ARL** (AB 2863, contracts entered/amended/extended on or after 1 July 2025) adds a remedy that should stop the room: **non-compliant goods are deemed an unconditional gift — full refund of everything ever charged.** And note what Amazon means for the founder-anonymity constraint: **personal liability survives website anonymity.**\n\n### 10. \"Worldwide\" + OFAC — and a sanctions clause copied from a template\niGrowFast LLC is a **US person**. OFAC prohibitions attach **wherever located**, and **liability is STRICT** — *\"'We didn't know the user was in Iran' is not a defense.\"* **IEEPA: greater of ~$377,700 per violation or twice the transaction value; criminal up to $1,000,000 and 20 years.** Morocco is clean; **the \"worldwide\" pitch is what creates the surface** — and it is an adjective, not a strategy. Then the template betrays you: **every ToS on the internet still lists Syria, and that is now affirmatively wrong.** **E.O. 14312** (30 Jun 2025, effective 1 Jul 2025) terminated the national emergency and revoked E.O.s 13338/13399/13460/13572/13573/13582; the **Syrian Sanctions Regulations, 31 CFR Part 542, were REMOVED from the CFR on 26 August 2025.** Publishing a stale embargo list is a false term in your own contract. Equally wrong in the other direction: describing **Russia, Belarus or Venezuela as embargoed** — they are **targeted/sectoral, not territorial**. Correct list: **Cuba, Iran, North Korea + Crimea/DNR/LNR (+Kherson, Zaporizhzhia)**. **Reference the SDN List, not a hand-maintained country list that goes stale.** And the clause is worthless without **T1.9**: screening at signup, periodic re-screening, the **50 Percent Rule**, and a **log** — *\"the record is what mitigates the penalty.\"*\n\n**Honourable mentions that did not make the ten but will still hurt:** Morocco **Art 59 prospection directe** (highest *complaint* probability of anything here); **ADA/Unruh** ($4,000/violation + fees, plaintiff-chosen forum — Wyoming's 10th Circuit does not protect you); the **accessiBe overlay trap** ($1M — never repeat a vendor's compliance claim); **Meta's missing UK DPF extension**; **Shopify PPA §9.14** vs. the core loop; **Woo §1.2.9** vs. commission pricing; **CIN capture** silently escalating every filing.\n\n---\n\n# 5. docs/website-recon/RAW-google.json:11:        "findings": "GOOGLE OAUTH VERIFICATION SURFACE — iGrowFast LLC\n\nHEADLINE: The Service Account architecture is the single most valuable compliance asset the product has. It mechanically removes iGrowFast from the OAuth verification and CASA surface entirely. Guard it. The website requirements below are cheap; the thing that costs five figures a year is a \"Connect your Google account\" button, and they do not have one. Do not add one.\n\n=====================================================================\n1. GOOGLE API SERVICES USER DATA POLICY\nSource: https://developers.google.com/terms/api-services-user-data-policy\nWorkspace overlay: https://developers.google.com/workspace/workspace-api-user-data-developer-policy\n\nDISCLOSURE REQUIREMENTS (verbatim):\n- \"publish a privacy policy that fully documents how your application interacts with user data\" and list that URL in the OAuth configuration.\n- \"Your Privacy Policy and all in-product privacy notifications should be accurate, comprehensive, and easily accessible.\"\n- Disclosures must be \"prominent and timely.\"\n- Must \"accurately represent\" three things: who requests the data, what data is requested, why it is requested.\n- If data uses expand beyond original disclosures: \"update your privacy policy and prompt the user to consent to any changes.\"\n- Minimum scope: \"Don't request access to information that you don't need. Only request access to the permissions necessary to implement your application's features.\"\n\nLIMITED USE — the four obligations (verbatim):\n(a) \"Limit your use of data to providing or improving user-facing features that are prominent in the requesting application's user interface.\"\n(b) \"Transfers of data are not allowed, except: To provide or improve your appropriate access or user-facing features that are visible and prominent in the requesting application's user interface and only with the user's consent; For security purposes (for example, investigating abuse); To comply with applicable laws; or, As part of a merger, acquisition, or sale of assets of the developer after obtaining explicit prior consent from the user.\"\n(c) \"Don't allow humans to read the data, unless: You first obtained the user's affirmative agreement to view specific messages, files, or other data...; It is necessary for security purposes...; It is necessary to comply with applicable law; or The data (including derivations) is aggregated and used for internal operations in accordance with applicable privacy and other jurisdictional legal requirements.\"\n(d) Prohibited: \"Transferring or selling user data to third parties like advertising platforms, data brokers, or any information resellers\"; \"Transferring, selling, or using user data for serving ads, including retargeting, personalized or interest-based advertising\"; \"Transferring, selling, or using user data to determine credit-worthiness or for lending purposes.\"\n\n*** THE AI/ML QUESTION — DEFINITIVE ANSWER ***\nThe AI/ML clause is REAL and is in the Workspace API User Data and Developer Policy, verbatim:\n\n  \"Transferring, selling, or using user data to create, train, or improve a machine learning or artificial intelligence model beyond that specific user's personalized model for the appropriate use case or user-facing feature.\"\n\n(Note: two independent summarization passes of the base policy page reported \"no AI/ML language exists.\" That is WRONG — the clause lives in the Workspace overlay policy, which is the one that governs Sheets. Anyone researching this who only reads the base policy will reach the wrong conclusion.)\n\nDoes Limited Use prohibit sending Google-user-data to OpenAI/Anthropic? NO — NOT CATEGORICALLY. It draws a hard line between TRAINING and INFERENCE:\n\n- TRAINING on Google user data → PROHIBITED outright, except a model personalized to that specific user for that specific feature. A cross-tenant model trained on many sellers' Sheets data = violation. No consent cures this; it is not one of the enumerated exceptions.\n- INFERENCE (sending data to an LLM to produce output for the user) → this is a \"transfer to a third party\" and is PERMITTED ONLY inside exception (b): the feature must be \"visible and prominent in the requesting application's user interface\" AND have \"the user's consent.\" A background/invisible AI enrichment pass over Sheets data fails the \"prominent in the UI\" test.\n\nTherefore three conditions must ALL hold to send Sheets-sourced data to OpenAI/Anthropic:\n  1. Zero-retention / no-training API tier, contractually confirmed (both OpenAI and Anthropic API defaults do not train on API data; the retention window and abuse-monitoring human review are the exposure).\n  2. The AI feature is prominent and visible in the app UI, not a silent backend process.\n  3. Seller consent is obtained and the privacy policy discloses the AI subprocessor by name.\nCondition (c) \"don't allow humans to read the data\" is the sleeper risk: AI-provider trust-and-safety staff reviewing flagged prompts would breach this. Zero-data-retention endpoints are the mitigation.\n\n*** CRITICAL SCOPING INSIGHT — the escape hatch ***\nLimited Use governs ONLY data obtained THROUGH Google APIs. COD order data that a seller types directly into iGrowFast, or imports by CSV, is NOT Google user data and Limited Use does not touch it at all. Only the Sheets-sync path is constrained. Architecturally: keep AI features on the native data path, and they are entirely outside this policy. If AI must touch Sheets-sourced rows, the three conditions above bind. This distinction is worth designing around — it is the difference between a governed and an ungoverned AI roadmap.\n\nCompliance with this policy is INDEPENDENT of verification status: per https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13464323, all apps must comply with the API Services User Data Policy regardless of whether they are exempt from verification. The Service Account exemption removes the verification process, NOT the Limited Use obligations.\n\n=====================================================================\n2. OAUTH APP VERIFICATION\nSources: https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13463073 ; https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13464321 ; https://developers.google.com/identity/protocols/oauth2/production-readiness/brand-verification\n\nTRIGGER (verbatim): \"Apps that request access to scopes categorized as sensitive or restricted must complete Google's OAuth app verification before being granted access.\" Non-sensitive-only apps are not required to verify (but still need brand verification to show a name/logo on the consent screen). Tier is auto-labelled in the Cloud Console scope picker when you add a scope — the Console is the authoritative tier oracle.\n\nHOMEPAGE (verbatim):\n- \"The homepage must be hosted on a verified domain you own\"\n- \"The homepage must accurately represent and identify your app or brand\"\n- \"The homepage must describe your app's functionality to its users. Your homepage can not be only a login page\"\n- \"publicly accessible, and not just accessible to your site's logged-in users\"\n- Must not be a Google Play Store or Facebook page link\n- \"You must add the link of your privacy policy to your homepage and this link should match the link you added on your OAuth consent screen configuration\"\n\nPRIVACY POLICY (verbatim):\n- \"The Privacy Policy should be hosted within the domain that hosts your homepage\" — SAME DOMAIN AS HOMEPAGE, MANDATORY\n- \"The Privacy Policy should be linked on your homepage so that users can find this information easily\"\n- \"The Privacy Policy must be linked from the OAuth consent screen on the Google API Console\"\n- \"must disclose how your app accesses, uses, stores, and/or shares Google user data\"\n- \"In-product privacy notifications must be prominently displayed in your app interface\"\n\nTERMS OF SERVICE: ToS URL is an authorized-domain-listed URI. Google's brand-verification requirements enumerate homepage, privacy policy, ToS, redirect URIs and JS origins as URIs whose top-level domains must all be listed in Authorized Domains and owned/verified. Google specifies far less normative detail for ToS than for the privacy policy — ToS is not the load-bearing document here. UNCERTAIN whether Google mandates a ToS at all for a non-Marketplace OAuth app; it is required as a field for Marketplace listings.\n\nAPP NAME / LOGO / BRANDING (verbatim):\n- \"All apps that access Google APIs must verify that they accurately represent their identity and intent\"\n- Consent screen must accurately display the app name and logo as configured on the Branding page; \"Any changes require resubmission and approval before taking effect.\"\n- \"Buttons or links that initiate an action on a Google product must follow the Google branding guidelines.\"\n\nDEMO VIDEO (required for sensitive/restricted scopes) (verbatim):\n- \"Show the end-to-end flow of your app including the OAuth grant process\"\n- \"Show the same application you have submitted for verification (including app name, branding)\"\n- \"Show the complete OAuth Consent Screen... The consent screen must also show the same exact scopes you are requesting\"\n- \"Must demonstrate the app functionalities that utilize the requested OAuth scopes\"\nPractical note for iGrowFast: the video must show the OAuth consent screen with matching branding. It does NOT require a founder on camera or any named person — a screen recording satisfies it. The founder-anonymity constraint is compatible with verification.\n\n=====================================================================\n3. SCOPE TIERS\nSource (authoritative, tier-labelled): https://developers.google.com/sheets/api/scopes ; https://developers.google.com/drive/api/guides/api-specific-auth\n\nDefinitions: Non-sensitive = \"provide the smallest scope of authorization access\"; Sensitive = \"provide access to specific Google User Data\"; Restricted = \"provide wide access to Google User Data.\"\n\nGOOGLE SHEETS (confirmed tiers):\n  https://www.googleapis.com/auth/spreadsheets            → SENSITIVE\n  https://www.googleapis.com/auth/spreadsheets.readonly   → SENSITIVE\n  https://www.googleapis.com/auth/drive.file              → NON-SENSITIVE  ← recommended\n  https://www.googleapis.com/auth/drive                   → RESTRICTED     ← triggers CASA\n  https://www.googleapis.com/auth/drive.readonly          → RESTRICTED     ← triggers CASA\n\nGOOGLE DRIVE (confirmed):\n  drive.file, drive.appdata → NON-SENSITIVE (\"recommended for most use cases\", \"per-file access\")\n  drive, drive.readonly, drive.metadata → RESTRICTED\nGoogle explicitly pushes migration to drive.file, which \"grants per-file and narrow access\" and avoids the restricted-scope verification burden.\n\nGOOGLE ANALYTICS (exact strings confirmed; TIER UNCERTAIN):\n  https://www.googleapis.com/auth/analytics\n  https://www.googleapis.com/auth/analytics.readonly\n  https://www.googleapis.com/auth/analytics.edit\n  https://www.googleapis.com/auth/analytics.manage.users\n  https://www.googleapis.com/auth/analytics.manage.users.readonly\n  https://www.googleapis.com/auth/analytics.provision\n  https://www.googleapis.com/auth/analytics.user.deletion\nUNCERTAIN: the OAuth scopes reference page does not render tier markers in fetched form, and I found no official page labelling Analytics scopes. They are NOT on any restricted-scope list I could locate, so they are Sensitive or Non-sensitive — NOT Restricted, therefore NOT CASA-triggering. Confirm the exact label in the Cloud Console scope picker, which auto-labels.\n\nGOOGLE ADS (exact string confirmed; TIER UNCERTAIN):\n  https://www.googleapis.com/auth/adwords\nUNCERTAIN: no official page I fetched labels its tier. Not on any restricted list found → not CASA-triggering. Confirm in Console. Separately, the Google Ads API imposes its OWN gate independent of OAuth verification: a developer token with Basic/Standard access, approved by a Google Ads API application review. That is a distinct process from OAuth verification and applies regardless.\n\n=====================================================================\n4. CASA (Cloud Application Security Assessment)\nSources: https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13465431 ; https://developers.google.com/identity/protocols/oauth2/production-readiness/restricted-scope-verification ; https://appdefensealliance.dev/casa\n\nWHEN REQUIRED (verbatim): \"Every app that requests access to Google users' restricted data and has the ability to access data from or through a third-party server must go through a security assessment.\"\nTIER THAT TRIGGERS IT: RESTRICTED SCOPES ONLY. Sensitive scopes do NOT trigger CASA — they trigger verification (video, homepage, privacy policy) but no security assessment. This is the single most important cost boundary in this whole document.\n\nFRAMEWORK: \"To standardize our security assessment we use the App Defense Alliance and the cloud application security assessment framework (CASA).\" \"CASA utilizes a risk-based, multi-tiered approach\"; apps are assigned \"either the AL1 or AL2 assurance level.\" CASA is \"based 100% on the OWASP ASVS; no proprietary requirements.\" Tier assignment is driven by data sensitivity, number of users per data type, risk tolerance, and risk indicators. \"All requirements must be satisfied for every level, the only difference between each level is the assessment method that applies.\"\n\nWHO PERFORMS IT: independent CASA-authorized labs/assessors accredited by the App Defense Alliance. Google does not perform it.\n\nCOST: Google's own statement (verbatim, https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13463817): \"Google does not charge the developer any fees for security assessment\" and \"The cost for such a service is agreed on between the developer and the assessor without any involvement from Google.\"\nUNCERTAIN — DOLLAR FIGURES: A search-engine synthesis surfaced \"$500 to $4,500 USD\" with \"2-3 weeks for tier-2, 4-6 weeks for tier-3,\" attributed to Google Workspace Marketplace docs. I fetched both Marketplace pages directly (developers.google.com/gsuite/marketplace/security-assessment and /workspace/marketplace/about-app-review) and NEITHER contains those figures — they only say \"If your app uses restricted scopes, it must also go through a security assessment.\" I therefore do NOT assert that range. The older widely-circulated \"$15,000–$75,000\" figure likewise does not appear in current official documentation. TREAT ALL PUBLISHED DOLLAR FIGURES AS UNVERIFIED; the only official position is that price is negotiated bilaterally with the assessor. Get direct quotes from multiple authorized labs.\n\nFREQUENCY (verbatim): \"All applications must be revalidated every year.\" And: \"apps must be reverified for compliance and complete a security assessment at least every 12 months after your assessor's Letter of Assessment (LOA) approval date.\" \"The required assurance level is dynamic and may increase based on changes in your user base or data-handling practices\" — i.e. growth alone can escalate the tier.\nNo free/self-service assessment tier is documented; both AL1 and AL2 are described as lab-tested/lab-verified.\n\nHOW TO AVOID CASA — three independent routes, any one suffices:\n  (i) Never request a restricted scope. Use drive.file (non-sensitive) or spreadsheets (sensitive). CASA is restricted-only.\n  (ii) Use a Service Account and no user OAuth (see §5) — removes the verification surface entirely.\n  (iii) Stay internal-only / under the exemptions in §5.\nFor iGrowFast, (i) and (ii) both already hold. They are not near CASA and should never go near it.\n\n=====================================================================\n5. *** THE CRITICAL ARCHITECTURE QUESTION — SERVICE ACCOUNT ***\nSource: https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13464323 ; https://developers.google.com/identity/protocols/oauth2/production-readiness/restricted-scope-verification ; https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13463817\n\nTHE EXEMPTION, VERBATIM (Google lists five cases where verification is NOT needed):\n  1. \"If the app is for your personal use (fewer than 100 users)\"\n  2. \"Apps in development/testing/staging mode are not subject to verification.\"\n  3. \"The app only accesses its own data (using a Service Account), and not user data (linked to a Google Account).\"\n  4. \"The app is only used by people in your Google Workspace or Cloud Identity organization\" (internal user type)\n  5. Workspace admin-trusted / Marketplace apps: \"An administrator can add a third-party app to the list of trusted apps in the Google Admin console.\"\n\nAnd verbatim from the restricted-scope verification page: \"If your app uses a service account to access only its own data, and it doesn't access any user data (linked to a Google Account), then you don't need to submit for verification.\"\n\nANSWER: YES — the Service Account model avoids OAuth verification and CASA. But the reasoning that makes it robust is MECHANICAL, not merely textual, and the textual exemption is a weaker support than it first appears. Both halves matter:\n\nTHE MECHANICAL ARGUMENT (strong, and the one to rely on):\nOAuth verification is a property of the OAuth CLIENT and its CONSENT SCREEN. A service account authenticates as ITSELF via a signed JWT assertion exchanged for a token bearing the SA's own identity. There is no consent screen, no user OAuth grant, no per-user token. Consequently:\n  - The \"unverified app\" warning screen cannot render — there is no consent screen to prepend it to.\n  - The 100-new-user cap cannot be consumed — that counter increments on user OAuth grants, of which there are zero.\n  - There is no verification submission to make — Google's verification intake is bound to an OAuth consent screen / brand configuration. A product with no consent screen has nothing to submit.\nCASA follows verification. No verification surface → no CASA. This holds regardless of which scopes the SA requests: an SA using the Sensitive `spreadsheets` scope still shows no consent screen, so scope tier is moot in the SA path. Tier only starts mattering the moment a consent screen exists.\n\nTHE TEXTUAL TENSION (be honest about this — it is the residual risk):\nThe exemption says \"accesses its own data ... and NOT user data (linked to a Google Account).\" When a Moroccan seller shares a spreadsheet they OWN with the SA's email, that spreadsheet IS data linked to a Google Account. A literal reading of the exemption is therefore ADVERSE — the SA is a Drive ACL grantee, not the owner. Google's drafting plainly contemplates the canonical case (SA owns/created the resource, e.g. its own BigQuery data), not the shared-file case.\nWeighing it: the mechanical argument dominates in practice — Google has no enforcement mechanism against a consent-screen-less SA, and Google's own FAQ blesses an adjacent shared-resource pattern verbatim: \"You can access data from your users' Google Cloud projects by creating a service account to represent your service, and then having your customers grant that service account appropriate access to their cloud data using IAM policies.\" That is precisely the share-with-the-SA topology, described approvingly. This is the strongest available authority for the pattern.\nVerdict: the SA + user-shared-sheet model does not require verification and does not incur CASA. Confidence: high on the mechanics, moderate on the letter of the exemption. This is not a five-figure annual assessment — it is nothing.\n\nLIMITS OF THE APPROACH — what breaks it:\n  a) DOMAIN-WIDE DELEGATION IS THE TRAP. If the SA is ever given DWD to impersonate users, it IS accessing user data on their behalf and verification applies for sensitive/restricted scopes. DWD is also unusable for iGrowFast's actual users: DWD requires a Google Workspace/Cloud Identity org admin. Sellers on consumer @gmail.com cannot grant it. The manual-share model is not merely acceptable — it is the ONLY option for consumer-Gmail sellers. Fortunate accident: the architecture is forced into the exempt shape.\n  b) ADDING A \"CONNECT YOUR GOOGLE ACCOUNT\" BUTTON destroys the exemption instantly. Per-user OAuth to `spreadsheets` (Sensitive) → full verification: homepage, same-domain privacy policy, demo video, Google review. Still no CASA (Sensitive ≠ Restricted) — unless someone reaches for `drive`/`drive.readonly`, which is Restricted and lands them in annual CASA. Treat a Google-account-connect button as a change requiring explicit sign-off.\n  c) LIMITED USE STILL BINDS. The exemption removes the PROCESS, not the POLICY. §1's AI/ML and transfer rules apply to Sheets-sourced data whether or not verification was ever required.\n  d) UX/OPERATIONAL COST: sellers must manually share each sheet with an unfriendly SA address (…iam.gserviceaccount.com). Support burden, not a compliance one.\n  e) SCOPE MECHANICS: `drive.file` covers only files the app created or the user opened via Picker — it does NOT cover a file manually shared with the SA. For the SA + shared-sheet flow the SA needs `spreadsheets`. Fine (no consent screen), but note this forecloses the \"just use drive.file\" answer IF they ever migrate to per-user OAuth. On that migration path they'd need `spreadsheets` (Sensitive, verification, no CASA) or a Picker-based drive.file flow (Non-sensitive, no verification) — the Picker route is the one to design toward if they ever leave the SA model.\n  f) KEY MANAGEMENT: the SA private key in Postgres Vault is now a crown-jewel credential granting access to every seller sheet ever shared with it. Compromise = cross-tenant breach of end-customer COD data with no per-user revocation granularity (sellers can individually unshare, but there is no token-revocation story). This is the real risk the SA model creates in exchange for the compliance relief. It is a GDPR/security exposure, not a Google-policy one — and given German-hosted infra processing EU-adjacent and Moroccan end-customer PII, it deserves separate attention.\n\n=====================================================================\n6. DOMAIN VERIFICATION\nSource: https://developers.google.com/identity/protocols/oauth2/production-readiness/brand-verification ; https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13464321\n\n- \"You must verify that you own all domains listed in your Authorized domains section.\"\n- Method: \"Verify the ownership of your authorized domains using Google Search Console.\" An account listed as project owner or editor on the GCP project must perform the verification; a Google Account with owner permissions must be associated with the API Console project.\n- Authorized Domains must list the top-level domains of every URI: homepage, privacy policy, ToS, redirect URIs, JavaScript origins.\n\nAPEX vs SUBDOMAIN — DIRECT ANSWER: verifying the apex is sufficient and subdomains need no separate verification. Google requires verification of \"the top private domain\" (the domain available for registration on a public suffix). Google's own example, verbatim: \"an OAuth consent screen that's configured with an application home page of https://sub.example.com/product asks the account holder to verify ownership of the example.com domain.\"\nFor iGrowFast: verify igrowfast.com in Search Console once. That single verification covers app.igrowfast.com and api.igrowfast.com. Homepage on the apex + app on the subdomain is exactly the topology Google's example describes. No conflict.\nFounder-anonymity note: Search Console verification is a private control-proof (DNS TXT record or file upload) between the domain owner and Google. It publishes nothing and requires no public name. DNS TXT is the cleanest method. Compatible with the no-founder-names constraint.\n\n=====================================================================\n7. CONSEQUENCES OF AN UNVERIFIED APP\nSource: https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/7454865 ; https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13463817\n\n- UNVERIFIED APP SCREEN: shown before the consent screen when the app uses sensitive/restricted scopes and has not completed verification.\n- 100-USER CAP (verbatim): \"New user cap\" of \"100 new users in total, after the app presents the unverified app screen.\" And: \"the user cap applies over the entire lifetime of the project, and it cannot be reset or changed.\" This is lifetime and permanent — not a rolling or resettable quota. Burning it on testing is unrecoverable without a new GCP project.\n- HARD FAILURE (verbatim): \"Failure to get your app verified before making requests to sensitive or restricted scopes will result in your project's 100 new-user cap eventually getting exhausted and Google sign-in being disabled for your users.\"\n- ADDITIONAL SIGNALS: Google Security Checkup flags the app as \"risky and unverified\"; users get notices about potential loss of functionality.\n- GRACE PERIOD: exists only in one narrow case — when Google RECLASSIFIES a scope as sensitive/restricted, existing apps get \"a grace period to go through verification before the unverified app screen and user cap are applied.\" There is no general grace period for new apps.\n- RESTRICTED SCOPES: not described as an instant hard block; they route through the same warning + cap mechanism, plus the mandatory security assessment before verification can complete.\n- Testing/dev is explicitly fine unverified: you can \"continue to build and test your app while waiting to complete verification.\"\n\nALL OF §7 IS INAPPLICABLE TO THE SA PATH — these consequences attach to the OAuth consent screen, which the SA architecture does not have. §7 is the description of what iGrowFast is avoiding, and the reason to keep avoiding it.\n\n=====================================================================\nNET ASSESSMENT FOR iGrowFast\n- Today, on the Service Account model: NO OAuth verification, NO CASA, NO 100-user cap, NO demo video. Zero Google review exposure.\n- The website requirements (homepage, same-domain privacy policy, Search Console verification) are NOT currently mandatory for Google, because nothing triggers verification. Build them anyway — they are cheap, they are required by GDPR/Moroccan reality independent of Google, and they are the prerequisite that must already exist on the day anyone proposes a Google-account-connect button. Retrofitting them under deadline is where projects fail.\n- The live, binding Google obligation today is LIMITED USE on Sheets-sourced data — specifically the prohibition on training AI/ML models on it. This constrains the AI roadmap NOW, with no verification trigger and no warning shot.\n- Highest-leverage decisions: (1) never request drive/drive.readonly; (2) never add per-user OAuth without deliberate sign-off; (3) never enable domain-wide delegation; (4) keep AI features on the native (non-Sheets) data path where Limited Use does not reach; (5) treat the SA key as the cross-tenant crown jewel it now is.\n- Two open items: exact Console tier labels for analytics/adwords scopes (confirm in the Cloud Console scope picker — it auto-labels), and real CASA pricing (only if they ever go restricted, which they should not).",

You have READ access to the live DB via `printf '%s' "SQL" | ~/.igrowfast/db.sh -t -A` (read-only; use it to dump live policy/function definitions — the LIVE catalog is the source of truth, NOT old migration files). DO NOT use --write; you author files only.

## FILE 1 — `supabase/migrations/20260729140000_business_owner_enum.sql`
Exactly: `ALTER TYPE public.user_role ADD VALUE IF NOT EXISTS 'business owner';` with a comment explaining it MUST be its own file/transaction (a new enum value cannot be used in the transaction that adds it). NO BEGIN/COMMIT in this file.

## FILE 2 — `supabase/migrations/20260729150000_business_owner_parity.sql`
Wrapped in BEGIN; SET LOCAL lock_timeout='5s'; … COMMIT; containing:
1. **Helper**: CREATE OR REPLACE `current_user_is_business_admin()` with `role IN ('super admin','admin','business owner')` — dump the live def first and change ONLY the array.
2. **Policies**: for EVERY policy from the live catalog matching 'super admin' (~44; query in inventory), regenerate with `'business owner'` added to the role array (handle both `ARRAY['super admin'::text,…]` and any `= 'super admin'` equality forms). Use `ALTER POLICY … USING (…) [WITH CHECK (…)]` with the FULL original expression text edited minimally — dump `qual`/`with_check` live per policy and splice. Storage.objects policies included. Preserve each policy's cmd/roles exactly. Emit them grouped by table with a count comment. IMPORTANT: generate this section by scripting over the live catalog output (write a scratch script if helpful), not by hand-typing 44 expressions — then paste the generated SQL into the migration.
3. **Functions**: for each of the 17 inventoried functions, dump the live def (`pg_get_functiondef`) and CREATE OR REPLACE with 'business owner' added wherever 'super admin' appears in a role check. Keep everything else byte-identical (CREATE OR REPLACE resets security_invoker on VIEWS only — these are functions, but preserve SECURITY DEFINER/search_path clauses exactly as dumped). If any occurrence of 'super admin' in a function is NOT a role check (e.g. a display string), leave it and note it in your report.
4. **One-per-business**: `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX users_one_business_owner_per_biz ON public.users ("DigiCom Business") WHERE role = 'business owner';`
5. **Protection trigger** on public.users: BEFORE UPDATE OF role OR DELETE; SECURITY DEFINER fn (search_path pinned, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC/anon/authenticated, TG_RELID check against public.users). Logic: acting user = auth.uid(); IF the OLD row has role='business owner' AND (TG_OP='DELETE' OR NEW.role IS DISTINCT FROM 'business owner') THEN allow ONLY IF auth.uid() IS NULL (service-role/system — edge fns enforce their own caller checks) OR the acting user's role='owner'; otherwise RAISE EXCEPTION with a clear French message ('Le propriétaire de l''entreprise ne peut pas être modifié par un pair.'). Also block INSERT/UPDATE that would create a SECOND business owner for a business? No — the unique index handles it; don't duplicate.
6. **Grants hygiene**: this migration creates one function — REVOKE EXECUTE from PUBLIC/anon/authenticated on it (STANDING RULE: box default ACL grants everything).

-- TKT-10 backfill: each existing business's EARLIEST super admin becomes its
-- business owner. Rule + the exact 7-user list previewed to and approved by
-- the owner 2026-07-29 (Bellora Bio, El Moda, Golden Design, moulay soltan,
-- Orelle, Ouzlim Bio, test env). Runs AFTER 20260729150000 (trigger + unique
-- index exist; the trigger only guards demotion/deletion, not promotion, and
-- the partial unique index enforces one owner per business).

BEGIN;
SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '5s';

UPDATE public.users u
SET role = 'business owner'
FROM (
    SELECT DISTINCT ON (u2."DigiCom Business") u2.id
    FROM public.users u2
    WHERE u2.role = 'super admin' AND u2."DigiCom Business" IS NOT NULL
    ORDER BY u2."DigiCom Business", u2.created_at ASC
) first_admin
WHERE u.id = first_admin.id;

COMMIT;

   371	      const { error: authError } = await supabaseClient.auth.admin.deleteUser(userId);
   372	      if (authError) throw authError;
   373	
   374	      const { error: profileError } = await supabaseClient
   375	        .from("users")
   376	        .delete()
   377	        .eq("id", userId);
   378	
   379	      if (profileError) throw profileError;
   380	
   140	        const pre = preValidate(body?.email);
   141	        if (!pre.ok) return json({ ok: false, deliverable: false, reason: pre.reason, suggestion: pre.suggestion }, 200);
   142	        const deep = await verifyDeliverable(pre.email);
   143	        if (!deep.ok) return json({ ok: false, deliverable: false, reason: deep.reason }, 200);
   144	        return json({ ok: true, deliverable: true }, 200);
   145	      } catch (e) {
   146	        return json({ ok: false, deliverable: false, reason: "error", detail: (e as Error).message }, 200);
   147	      }
   148	    }
   149	
   150	    const targetRole = String(body?.target_role ?? "");
   151	    if (!ALLOWED_TARGET_ROLES.includes(targetRole)) return json({ error: "Invalid target role" }, 400);
   152	    if (targetRole === "business owner") {
   153	      return json({ error: "Business owner cannot be invited" }, 403);
   154	    }
   155	    if (me.role === "admin" && targetRole === "super admin") {
   156	      return json({ error: "Admins cannot invite super admins" }, 403);
   157	    }
   158	
   159	    // Email is OPTIONAL — store it only if well-formed. Creation is NEVER blocked by the email.
   160	    const raw = String(body?.email ?? "").trim().toLowerCase();
   161	    let email = null;
   162	    if (raw) { const pre = preValidate(raw); if (pre.ok) email = pre.email; }
    33	-- R2 — users: column-level UPDATE grant + privileged-column guard
    34	-- ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    35	REVOKE UPDATE ON public.users FROM authenticated;
    36	GRANT UPDATE (
    37	  name, username, phone, address,
    38	  avatar_url, avatar_config,
    39	  bank_name, bank_account_holder, bank_rib, cin_number,
    40	  courier_parcel_fragile, courier_parcel_open, courier_parcel_try_on,
    41	  updated_at
    42	) ON public.users TO authenticated;
    43	
    44	CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.guard_users_self_privileged_cols()
    45	RETURNS trigger
    46	LANGUAGE plpgsql SET search_path = public, pg_temp
    47	AS $$
    48	BEGIN
    49	  -- Exempt non-end-user writers (see header). Only gate real self-edits.
    50	  IF current_user <> 'authenticated' THEN RETURN NEW; END IF;
    51	  IF public.current_user_is_business_admin() THEN RETURN NEW; END IF;
    52	
    53	  IF ( NEW.role              IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.role
    54	    OR NEW."DigiCom Business" IS DISTINCT FROM OLD."DigiCom Business"
    55	    OR NEW.permissions       IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.permissions
    56	    OR NEW.commission_price  IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.commission_price
    57	    OR NEW.commission_basis  IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.commission_basis
    58	    OR NEW.salary_amount     IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.salary_amount
    59	    OR NEW.payment_type      IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.payment_type
    60	    OR NEW.active            IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.active ) THEN
    61	    RAISE EXCEPTION 'privileged column change not permitted on self-update'
    62	      USING ERRCODE = '42501';
    63	  END IF;
    64	  RETURN NEW;
    65	END $$;
    66	
    67	DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS trg_guard_users_self_privileged_cols ON public.users;
    68	CREATE TRIGGER trg_guard_users_self_privileged_cols
    69	  BEFORE UPDATE ON public.users FOR EACH ROW
    70	  EXECUTE FUNCTION public.guard_users_self_privileged_cols();
