  ✓ Reconcile altered policies/functions/view against source and live database
  ✓ Analyze trigger, uniqueness, transactions, idempotency, and locks
  ✓ Report adversarial findings and unverifiable items
codex
FAIL

### Findings

1. **Critical — unauthorized business-owner assignment remains possible.**  
   [FILE2 §2](/Users/ismailelqorchi/Desktop/iGrowFast/New%20Website/iGrowFast/supabase/migrations/20260729150000_business_owner_parity.sql:188) lets any `super admin`, `admin`, or `business owner` satisfy `"Admins can insert users"` without constraining the inserted role. [The protection trigger](/Users/ismailelqorchi/Desktop/iGrowFast/New%20Website/iGrowFast/supabase/migrations/20260729150000_business_owner_parity.sql:2876) only examines `OLD.role` and does not fire on `INSERT` or promotion into `business owner`. With the stated table privileges, a peer admin can delete/reinsert an ordinary profile as `business owner`, particularly between FILE2 and backfill or for a business without an owner. Edge-function guards do not cover direct PostgREST table writes.  
   **Fix:** add a `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE` guard that permits transitions into `business owner` only when `auth.uid() IS NULL` or the acting database user row has role `owner`.

2. **High — a business owner can have no business.**  
   [FILE2 §4](/Users/ismailelqorchi/Desktop/iGrowFast/New%20Website/iGrowFast/supabase/migrations/20260729150000_business_owner_parity.sql:2851) permits unlimited `business owner` rows where `"DigiCom Business" IS NULL`; PostgreSQL unique indexes treat nulls as distinct by default. Such a row passes the admin helper but has inconsistent tenant scope. The platform `owner` role does not collide because it is excluded by the partial-index predicate. [PostgreSQL unique-index semantics](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/indexes-unique.html).  
   **Fix:** add `CHECK (role <> 'business owner' OR "DigiCom Business" IS NOT NULL)`; `NULLS NOT DISTINCT` is weaker because it would still permit one invalid null owner.

3. **Medium — FILE2 holds broad `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` locks longer than necessary.**  
   The 44 `ALTER POLICY` statements cover 16 tables and appear before roughly 140 KB of function/index/trigger/view work. PostgreSQL implements `ALTER POLICY` with `AccessExclusiveLock`, held until transaction end. `lock_timeout = '5s'` limits acquisition waits, not the time already-acquired locks block reads and writes. A later contested lock can therefore block previously locked tables for up to five seconds before rollback. [PostgreSQL policy implementation](https://github.com/postgres/postgres/blob/master/src/backend/commands/policy.c).  
   **Fix:** move the large function section before the policy block and keep policy/index/trigger/view DDL in the shortest possible tail transaction; preflight `users` index-build duration.

4. **Low — `UPDATE OF role` has a documented future-trigger bypass.**  
   Direct `UPDATE users SET role=...` fires correctly, and FK-cascaded deletes fire the row-level DELETE trigger. But an earlier `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger can modify `NEW.role` during an update whose target list omitted `role`; PostgreSQL explicitly says column-specific triggers do not fire for such changes. No current repo trigger was found that mutates the role this way. [PostgreSQL trigger behavior](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createtrigger.html).  
   **Fix:** use `BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE` and retain the internal `OLD.role`/`NEW.role` comparison.

5. **Low — FILE2 is not replay-idempotent.**  
   FILE1 is idempotent through `ADD VALUE IF NOT EXISTS`. FILE2 replays successfully until `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX users_one_business_owner_per_biz`, which fails if the index already exists; the transaction then rolls back.  
   **Fix:** use a guarded definition check, or at minimum `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` with a separate assertion that an existing same-named index has the expected definition.

6. **Low — the helper is replaced twice.**  
   `current_user_is_business_admin()` appears at [line 31](/Users/ismailelqorchi/Desktop/iGrowFast/New%20Website/iGrowFast/supabase/migrations/20260729150000_business_owner_parity.sql:31) and again at [line 233](/Users/ismailelqorchi/Desktop/iGrowFast/New%20Website/iGrowFast/supabase/migrations/20260729150000_business_owner_parity.sql:233). They are identical today, so this is harmless but creates a future overwrite trap.  
   **Fix:** remove the second definition.

### Verified clean

- **Enum transaction:** FILE1 contains only the enum addition. FILE2 references the value in a later transaction. This is correct provided they remain separate `psql` invocations and are not combined with `--single-transaction`. PostgreSQL forbids using a newly added enum value before the adding transaction commits. [PostgreSQL `ALTER TYPE`](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/15/sql-altertype.html).
- **Casts:** policy arrays use `text`; view arrays use `user_role`; uncast literals inside enum comparisons and `check_user_role(text[])` calls resolve from context. No mismatched cast found.
- **Policy reconciliation:** actual count is **44, not 46**: 38 `public` plus six `storage.objects` policies—three `demand_media_*` and three `stamps_*`. All 44 de-splice byte-for-byte to the captured live-catalog JSON. No missing, extra, truncated, or double-spliced statement.
- **Spot checks:** command/roles/clause placement matched for `lock_select`, both courier-note policies, `ga_connections_update_own_business`, `mediabuyer_demands_select_scoped`, `mediabuyer_payment_txns_tenant_insert`, `user_invitations_admin_revoke`, both reviewed `users` policies, `demand_media_delete_scoped`, and `stamps_write_business_admins`.
- **Functions:** all 16 edited definitions de-splice byte-for-byte to the captured `pg_get_functiondef()` files. `SECURITY DEFINER`, `STABLE`, language, return signature, and `search_path` clauses are preserved. Each contains exactly one intended splice. `seller_roster_stats` correctly remains unedited because its occurrence is comment-only.
- **Existing-function ACLs:** `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` preserves ownership and permissions. [PostgreSQL `CREATE FUNCTION`](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createfunction.html).
- **Demotion/delete protection:** direct role updates and row deletes are blocked for authenticated peers; platform `owner` passes; `auth.uid() IS NULL` passes as designed; `TG_RELID`, pinned `search_path`, `SECURITY DEFINER`, and EXECUTE revocation are present.
- **View:** `security_barrier=true` is explicit. `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` preserves ownership and grants; an incompatible output column name/order/type causes the entire transaction to fail rather than silently change the contract. [PostgreSQL `CREATE VIEW`](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createview.html).
- **Static lexical check:** balanced strings, dollar quotes, and parentheses.

### Static review could not verify

- Live catalog drift after the captured `.foreman/scratch/tkt10-*` dumps; sandbox networking prevented a fresh box query.
- The current `whatsapp.profiles` output columns/types and ACLs against the live object.
- `public.users` owner, `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`, and whether the new SECDEF function owner bypasses RLS for the acting-role lookup.
- Actual table sizes, lock contention, and index-build duration.
- Runtime compilation and behavior under impersonated `owner`, peer admin, authenticated user, service-role, FK cascade, and direct REST insert.
